//! CLI security reporting; compile/test evidence share one analyzer.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#191-deploy-rollback-and-release-inspection-dx12 — gates lead; deploy advisory questions are opt-in.
use super::*;
use snapback4_lang::security::{self,Report};
mod http;
mod record;
mod presence;
pub(super) mod witnesses;
#[cfg(test)] mod derived_tests;
pub(super) use presence::remember as remember_presence;
pub(super) use record::{remember,capture};
pub(super) fn unauthenticated(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)])->bool {
    match http::run(project,bound,sources){Ok(_)=>true,Err(why)=>{eprintln!("security HTTP failure: {why}");false}}
}
pub(super) fn report(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],options:&Options)->Report{
    report_with_reviewed(project,bound,sources,options,&Default::default())
}
fn report_with_reviewed(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],options:&Options,reviewed:&std::collections::BTreeSet<String>)->Report{
    let augmented=if options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){security::derived::augmented(sources,bound)}else{bound.clone()};
    let bound=&augmented;
    // Reviewed alternatives remain in the report and full-test evidence, but
    // cannot spend the production replay budget ahead of other obligations.
    let replay=if reviewed.is_empty(){std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(bound)}else{
        let mut replay=bound.clone();replay.invariants.retain(|i|!security::derived::is_derived(i)||!reviewed.contains(&i.site.to_string()));std::borrow::Cow::Owned(replay)
    };
    let replay=replay.as_ref();
    let mut selection=options.clone();
    selection.json=true; // Internal fixture loading must not print a second check transcript.
    let selected_fixture=record::selected_fixture(project,options);
    if selected_fixture.is_dir(){selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),selected_fixture.to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
    let inputs=match selection.flags.get("fixture"){
        Some(path)=>match super::test_fixture::snapshot(std::path::Path::new(path)){Ok(snapshot)=>Some(snapshot),Err(why)=>{let mut report=security::analyze_rules(bound);security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);report.campaign_coverage.insert(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));return report;}},None=>None,
    };
    if let Some(snapshot)=&inputs{selection.flags.insert("fixture".into(),snapshot.0.to_string_lossy().into_owned());}
    let cached=record::matching(project,&selection,sources);
    let known_failure=cached.as_ref().is_some_and(|record|record.violations.iter().any(|v|v.reachability.is_reachable()&&replay.invariants.iter().any(|i|!security::derived::is_derived(i)&&i.site.to_string()==v.invariant_site)))&&!options.flags.contains_key("security-full");
    let mut campaign=if options.flags.contains_key("security-full"){security::challenges::Campaign::default()}else{if options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){security::challenges::Campaign::work_bounded(3072)}else{security::challenges::Campaign::work_bounded(512)}};
    if known_failure {campaign.work_remaining=Some(0);campaign.unsupported.insert("matching full-test refutations retained; bounded resampling skipped; repair and rerun snapback4 test".into());}
    // An explicitly selected fixture remains the initial-state assumption.
    // Static generation runs only on the fixture-free path; both share this
    // one bounded campaign with all subsequent concrete exploration.
    let mut report=if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture")||known_failure||options.flags.contains_key("security-deploy"){
        security::analyze_rules(bound)
    }else{security::analyze_with_campaign(bound,&mut campaign)};
    security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);
    if !replay.invariants.is_empty()&&!known_failure{
        if selection.flags.contains_key("fixture"){
            match super::test_fixture::engine_at(project,replay,&selection,1_800_000_000_000){
                Ok(fixture)=>if bound.schema.tables.keys().any(|t|fixture.store().rows(t).is_ok_and(|rs|!rs.is_empty())){security::challenges::fixture_observers(replay,&fixture,&mut campaign)}else{
                    // An empty starter directory selects generation, just as
                    // no fixture does. Give the direct contradiction its turn
                    // before empty-start mutation permutations spend the cap.
                    let explored=security::analyze_with_campaign(replay,&mut campaign);
                    if reviewed.is_empty(){report=explored;}
                    security::add_source_notes(&mut report,sources,bound);
                    security::challenges::generated_campaign(replay,&mut campaign,false)
                },
                Err(why)=>{campaign.unsupported.insert(format!("fixture reachability not covered: {why}"));}
            }
        }else{security::challenges::generated_campaign(replay,&mut campaign,false);}
    }
    let sites=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&!campaign.violations.iter().any(|c|c.invariant_site==v.invariant.site.to_string()&&c.reachability.is_reachable())).map(|v|v.invariant.site.to_string()).collect();
    let campaign=security::challenges::run_selected_after(replay,Some(&sites),campaign);
    security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&campaign);
    report.notes.extend(presence::notes(project,bound,None,sources).unwrap_or_else(|why|vec![snapback4_lang::Diagnostic::new(&Default::default(),"N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING",why,"repair the unreadable generated presence baseline").guide("security")]));
    if let Some(record)=cached {security::challenges::apply(&mut report,&security::challenges::Campaign{violations:record.violations,..Default::default()});}
    let mut canonical=security::analyze_rules(bound);
    security::add_source_notes(&mut canonical,sources,bound);
    canonical.notes.extend(report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code=="N_PRESENCE_LOOSENING").cloned());
    report.notes=canonical.notes;
    report
}
pub fn audit(options:&Options)->i32{
    let project=project_or_exit();
    let sources=match project.sources(){Ok(s)=>s,Err(e)=>{eprintln!("audit: {e}");return 2;}};
    let bound=compiled_or_exit(snapback4_lang::compile(&sources),options.json);
    // Audit is a permission map plus bounded evidence; `test` owns full campaigns.
    let report=report(&project,&bound,&sources,options);
    let dispositions=match super::security_reviews::review(&project,&sources,&report.notes,None){Ok(d)=>d,Err(error)=>{eprintln!("audit: E_SECURITY_NOTES: {error}");return 2;}};
    if options.json {let mut value=serde_json::to_value(&report).unwrap();value["securityReviews"]=serde_json::to_value(&dispositions).unwrap();println!("{value}");}
    else {let mut human=report.clone();human.notes.retain(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review);print!("{}",human.text_with_all(options.flags.contains_key("all")));super::security_reviews::print(&dispositions,&report.notes);}
    if report.refuted(){1}else{0}
}
pub(super) fn exposure(report:&Report){
    let broad=report.exposures.iter().filter(|e|e.effects.get("read").is_some_and(|r|r=="allow"||r.starts_with("public"))).count();
    eprintln!("security exposure: {} tables, {} conditional audiences, {broad} broadly readable tables; run snapback4 audit for exact role/column predicates",report.exposures.len(),report.roles.len());
}
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#18-wave-26-security-decisions-dx9 — report enforcement facts without claiming complete security.
pub(super) fn enforced(report:&Report,bound:&Bound){println!("{}",enforced_text(report,bound));}
pub(in crate::verbs) fn enforced_text(report:&Report,bound:&Bound)->String {
    let roles=report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code=="N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION").count();
    let columns=bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(_,t)|t.column_reads.len()).sum::<usize>();
    let uploads=bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|t.columns.values()).filter(|t|t.contains_asset()).count();
    format!("check: enforced by the language — {uploads} typed upload columns with owner-bound placement; {columns} column audiences; {roles} possible self-assignable role paths detected (bounded analysis)")
}
pub(super) fn summary(report:&Report,verbose:bool){
    let proved=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Proved).count();
    let failures=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let refuted=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted).count();
    let rule_enforced=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Unproved&&v.direct_rule==security::Status::Proved).count();
    eprintln!("security: {proved} proved, {refuted} refuted; {rule_enforced} enforced by rule (additional paths not proved); {} other unproved ({} failing)",report.invariants.len()-proved-refuted-rule_enforced,failures.len());
    for v in &failures {
        let c=v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
        eprintln!("  FIX {}: {} via {} as {} ({}); tighten enforcement to the declared intent, then run snapback4 test",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.target_label(),c.operation,c.viewer,serde_json::to_value(&c.reachability).unwrap().as_str().unwrap());
    }
    let supplemental=report.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|!c.reachability.is_reachable())).count();
    if supplemental>0 {eprintln!("  {supplemental} seed/synthetic observations (non-failing)");}
    if verbose {let mut human=report.clone();human.notes.retain(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review);eprint!("{}",human.text_with_all(true));}
    else if !report.invariants.is_empty()||!report.campaign_coverage.is_empty(){eprintln!("  next: snapback4 test; {} coverage gaps (check --verbose/JSON keeps every reason)",report.campaign_coverage.len());}
    if !report.exposures.is_empty(){eprintln!("  review audiences and {} uncovered exposures: snapback4 audit",report.uncovered.len());}
}

/// A rule-changing preflight demands a current campaign, without pretending
/// finite tests or undeclared intent certify a deployment.
pub(super) fn release_loop(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],prior:Option<&Bound>,options:&Options)->Result<Option<String>,String>{
    let changed=match prior {
        None=>true,
        Some(old)=>serde_json::to_vec(&old.schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.schema).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?
            ||serde_json::to_vec(&old.invariants).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.invariants).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?
            ||serde_json::to_vec(&old.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?!=serde_json::to_vec(&bound.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?,
    };
    if !changed{return Ok(None);}
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.004#153-rule-changing-deployment-review — review is prominent, strictness is explicit and failure-origin aware.
    let started=std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut preflight=options.clone();if !options.flags.contains_key("staging")||options.flags.contains_key("strict"){preflight.flags.insert("security-deploy".into(),String::new());}
    let decisions=super::security_reviews::ReviewSnapshot::capture(project)?;
    let derived=security::derived::notes(sources,bound);
    // A legacy rationale cannot skip the replay that establishes its current
    // witness. Individual alternatives are applied only after actual evidence.
    let mut report=report_with_reviewed(project,bound,sources,&preflight,&Default::default());
    report.notes.extend(presence::notes(project,bound,prior,sources)?);
    if super::proof_source::sources(project,options,"schema")?!=sources{return Err("selected Quarry sources changed during deployment security preflight".into());}
    let mut evidence=witnesses::collect(project,bound,sources,&report.notes,true,options);
    witnesses::apply_report(sources,bound,&report,&report.notes,&mut evidence);
    if super::proof_source::sources(project,options,"schema")?!=sources{return Err("selected Quarry sources changed during deployment witness replay; rerun deploy".into());}
    let dispositions=decisions.dispositions_with_evidence(sources,&report.notes,&evidence);
    super::security_reviews::remember_families(project,sources,&report.notes);
    decisions.ensure_current(project)?;
    let acknowledged=derived.iter().filter(|n|report.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).any(|(r,d)|r.site==n.site&&r.code==n.code&&r.message==n.message&&d.acknowledged)).map(|n|n.site.to_string()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>();
    let pending=super::security_reviews::pending(&dispositions);
    let witnessed=|v:&&security::Verdict|v.status==security::Status::Refuted&&v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c|c.reachability.is_reachable()&&(!security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant)||super::security_reviews::witness::concrete(&witnesses::evidence(sources,bound,&v.invariant,c))));
    let fixes=report.invariants.iter().filter(witnessed).filter(|v|!security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant)||!acknowledged.contains(&v.invariant.site.to_string())).count();
    if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") { eprintln!("security deploy campaign: {} ms; bounded replay, remaining paths retain coverage gaps",started.elapsed().as_millis()); }
    let scope=release_scope(bound,prior,sources);
    let review=options.flags.contains_key("review")||options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
    let mut notice=String::new();
    for v in report.invariants.iter().filter(witnessed){
        let c=v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
        if security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant)&&acknowledged.contains(&v.invariant.site.to_string()){
            if review {notice.push_str(&format!("  REVIEWED derived {}: individual specification decision retained\n",v.invariant.target_label()));} continue;
        }
        notice.push_str(&format!("  FIX {} {}: refuted via {} as {} ({:?}); repair enforcement to match intent, then run snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.target_label(),c.operation,c.viewer,c.reachability));
        if !security::derived::is_derived(&v.invariant) {
            if v.invariant.target=="write"&&v.invariant.column.is_some()&&c.effect=="insert" {
                notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if broader creation is deliberate, replace this assertion with `invariant {}.{} update only {}`; then run snapback4 test. Authored contradictions cannot be cleared with --ack-security\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table,v.invariant.column.as_ref().unwrap(),v.invariant.predicate));
            }else {
                notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if this observed audience is deliberate, revise the authored assertion here to match that specification, then run snapback4 test; --ack-security applies only to derived questions, never an authored contradiction\n",v.invariant.site));
            }
        }
        let aggregates=bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter(|m|m.source==v.invariant.table).map(|m|m.target.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if c.observations.is_some()&&!aggregates.is_empty()&&v.invariant.target=="read"&&v.invariant.column.is_none() {
            notice.push_str(&format!("  DERIVED {}: a separately readable aggregate changes in the paired observation; tightening the source read rule alone cannot clear this disclosure\n",aggregates.join(", ")));
            let field=v.invariant.predicate.split('.').skip(1).filter_map(|part|part.split(|c:char|!c.is_alphanumeric()&&c!='_').next()).find(|field|bound.schema.table(&v.invariant.table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(*field)).is_some_and(|kind|matches!(kind.required(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)));
            if let Some(field)=field {notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if aggregate counts are intentionally public, replace the whole-row assertion with `invariant {}.{} read only {}` for this protected field and independently declare any other private fields; otherwise restrict the aggregate audience; rerun snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table,field,v.invariant.predicate));}
        }else if v.invariant.target=="read"&&v.invariant.column.is_none() {
            for (file,text) in sources {
                let doc=snapback4_lang::parser::parse_document(file,text).0;
                if let Some(table)=doc.tables.iter().find(|t|t.name==v.invariant.table) {
                    if let Some(rule)=table.rules.iter().find(|r|r.effect==snapback4_lang::ast::Effect::Read) {
                        let expanded=bound.refusal_sites.get(&(v.invariant.table.clone(),"$read-rule".into()));
                        if expanded.is_some() {
                            notice.push_str(&format!("  SOURCE {}: preserve `{}` and its parent lifetime/authority; if this audience is unintended, repair the parent read rule before rerunning snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.table,snapback4_lang::bind::spell_internals(&snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value))));
                        }else {
                            notice.push_str(&format!("  SOURCE {}: retain the current audience and enforce this declared intent with `read <- ({}) and ({})`; rerun snapback4 test\n",v.invariant.table,snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value),v.invariant.predicate));
                        }
                        let predicate=expanded.cloned().unwrap_or_else(||snapback4_lang::print::expression(&rule.value));
                        notice.push_str(&format!("  INTENT {}: if every reader admitted by the current read rule is deliberately allowed, replace this assertion with `invariant {}.read only {predicate}`; then run snapback4 test and deploy again. This is an authored specification change, not --ack-security\n",v.invariant.site,v.invariant.table));
                    }
                }
            }
        }

    }
    let mut notes=report.notes.iter().filter(|n|n.code!="N_SECURITY_INTENT"&&scope.iter().any(|(file,start,end)|n.site.file==*file&&n.site.line>=*start&&n.site.line<*end)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    notes.sort_by_key(|n|crate::note_rank::summary_order(&n.code));
    let count=notes.len();
    // Never suppress a gate or write-authority question behind the display cap.
    for (i,n) in notes.into_iter().enumerate(){if (review||crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Fix)&&crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review&&(i<5||security::note_priority(&n.code)<=3||crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Fix){notice.push_str(&format!("  REVIEW {} {}: {}\n    {}\n",n.site,n.code,n.message,n.rewrite));}}
    if review&&count>5 {notice.push_str(&format!("  {count} changed-scope notes in deploy --check --json\n"));}
    let selected=report.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).filter(|(n,d)|d.gated || d.witness.is_some()&&!d.acknowledged || n.code!="N_SECURITY_INTENT" && scope.iter().any(|(file,start,end)| n.site.file==*file&&n.site.line>=*start&&n.site.line<*end)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let complete_family=dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.family.is_some()).count()==selected.iter().filter(|(_,d)|d.family.is_some()).count();
    let scoped_notes=selected.iter().map(|(n,_)|(*n).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let scoped_dispositions=selected.iter().map(|(_,d)| {let mut d=(*d).clone(); if !complete_family {d.family=None;} d}).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    // A scoped deploy cannot silently acknowledge unseen family members. It
    // prints their individual commands; ordinary check offers the whole family.
    notice.push_str(&super::security_reviews::render_mode(&scoped_dispositions,&scoped_notes,review));
    let test=release_notice(project,bound,sources,options);
    let advice=scoped_dispositions.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&!d.acknowledged&&d.witness.is_none()).count();
    let witnessed=scoped_dispositions.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&!d.acknowledged&&d.witness.is_some()).count();
    notice.push_str(&format!("{}; {witnessed} witnessed questions; {advice} advisories (don't block): deploy --review (or check --review)\n",enforced_text(&report,bound).replacen("check:","security:",1)));
    if review||test.contains("run `snapback4 test")||test.contains("FAILED") {notice.push_str(&format!("  {test}\n"));}
    let gates=dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged).count();
    let verdict=format!("security FIX {fixes}; {gates} gates; {witnessed} witnessed questions; {advice} advisories (don't block): deploy --review");
    Ok(Some(serde_json::json!({"notice":notice,"verdict":verdict,"fixes":fixes,"pending":pending,"changedNotes":count,"report":report,"securityReviews":dispositions}).to_string()))
}
fn age(timestamp:Option<i64>,now:i64)->String{
    timestamp.map(|t|format!("at {t} ms since Unix epoch, age {}s",now.saturating_sub(t).max(0)/1000)).unwrap_or_else(||"time/age unknown (legacy record)".into())
}
fn release_notice(project:&Project,bound:&Bound,sources:&[(String,String)],options:&Options)->String{
    let selection=options.flags.get("schema").map(|s|format!(" --source {s:?}")).unwrap_or_default();
    let fixture=options.flags.get("fixture").map(|s|format!(" --fixture {s:?}")).unwrap_or_default();
    let now=snapback4_core::now_ms();
    let matching=record::matching(project,options,sources);
    let matches=matching.is_some();
    match matching.or_else(||record::latest(project)){
        Some(record)=>{
            let result=if !record.complete{"incomplete"}else if record.run_violations.unwrap_or(record.violations.len())>0{"FAILED"}else{"completed, no observed violation"};
            let current=if matches{"matches selected source/fixtures/CLI"}else{"STALE: source, fixtures or CLI differ; does not qualify selected release"};
            let next=if matches&&record.complete&&record.run_violations.unwrap_or(record.violations.len())==0{"matching test campaign recorded (finite, not proof)".into()}else{format!("run `snapback4 test{selection}{fixture}` before deployment")};
            format!("last test: {result}, {}; {current}; {} retained reachable refutations, {} coverage gaps; last completed test {}; {next}",age(record.recorded_at,now),record.violations.len(),record.coverage_gaps,age(record.last_completed_at,now))
        },
        None=>format!("last test: NEVER RECORDED; run `snapback4 test{selection}{fixture}` before deployment; no matching completed campaign for this source, fixtures and CLI; {} independent invariants",bound.invariants.len()),
    }
}

// SEC7 owns ordinary check ranking/gates; this scope and output belong to ROLL.
fn release_scope(bound:&Bound,prior:Option<&Bound>,sources:&[(String,String)])->Vec<(String,u32,u32)> {
    let mut scope=Vec::new();
    fn normalized(p:&impl serde::Serialize)->serde_json::Value {
        fn clear(v:&mut serde_json::Value){match v{serde_json::Value::Object(o)=>{o.remove("site");for v in o.values_mut(){clear(v);}},serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{clear(v)},_=>()}}
        let mut v=serde_json::to_value(p).unwrap();clear(&mut v);v
    }
    for(file,text)in sources {
        let doc=snapback4_lang::parser::parse_document(file,text).0;
        let mut starts=text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_,s)|!s.is_empty()&&!s.starts_with(char::is_whitespace)&&!s.starts_with("--")).map(|(i,_)|i as u32+1).collect::<Vec<_>>();starts.sort();
        let mut add=|line:u32|scope.push((file.clone(),line,starts.iter().copied().find(|n|*n>line).unwrap_or(u32::MAX)));
        for t in &doc.tables {if prior.is_none_or(|old|old.schema.tables.get(&t.name).map(normalized)!=bound.schema.tables.get(&t.name).map(normalized)){add(t.site.line);}}
        for p in &doc.programs {
            let next=bound.programs.iter().find(|b|b.program.name==p.name);
            let old=prior.and_then(|b|b.programs.iter().find(|b|b.program.name==p.name));
            if next.map(|b|normalized(&b.program))!=old.map(|b|normalized(&b.program)){add(p.site.line);}
        }
    }scope
}
pub(super) fn print_release(summary:Option<&String>){if let Some(s)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()){eprint!("{}",s["notice"].as_str().unwrap_or(""));}}
pub(super) fn release_verdict(summary:Option<&String>)->String {summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok()).and_then(|s|s["verdict"].as_str().map(str::to_owned)).unwrap_or_else(||"security unchanged".into())}

pub(super) fn enforce_release(summary:Option<&String>,options:&Options)->Result<(),String>{
    let Some(value)=summary.and_then(|s|serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(s).ok())else{return Ok(())};
    if !options.flags.contains_key("staging")||options.flags.contains_key("strict"){
        if value["fixes"].as_u64().unwrap_or(0)>0{return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy refuses reachable FIX; repair the named rule and rerun snapback4 test; for a derived candidate only, run check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification rationale>' using the reported rule ID".into())}
        if value["pending"].as_bool()==Some(true){return Err("E_SECURITY_REVIEW: production deploy requires individual security review; run each reported check --ack-security ID --reason '<specification decision>', or enforce the named intent in source".into())}
    }Ok(())
}
        return 1;
    }
    // A native job's module is built here too (LLP 3000.005): the schema's
    // Rust types written, cargo run, the module checked against the jobs.
    let native = match crate::native::build(&project, &bound) {
        Ok(built) => built,
        Err(problem) => {
            if options.json {
                println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": problem.code, "message": problem.message, "rewrite": problem.rewrite, "guide": "native" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] }));
            } else {
                eprintln!("{problem}\n");
                eprintln!("check: 1 problem");
            }
            return 1;
        }
    };
    if let Err(error) = residency::remember(&project, &bound) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write residency facts: {error}")); return 2; }
    let acquisition = if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
        match residency::at_check(&project, &bound, options) {
            Ok(report) => report,
            Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("residency measurement failed: {error}")); return 1; }
        }
    } else { None };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#132-reviewed-notes — every failed check preserves review evidence, including independent policy refutations.
    let reviewed = if security.refuted() {
        note_reviews::Review { new: bound.notes.clone(), acknowledged: Vec::new() }
    } else { match note_reviews::review(&project, &compiled_sources, &bound.notes, &reviewable_notes, options.flags.contains_key("ack-notes")) {
        Ok(review) => review,
        Err(error) => {
            if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "problems": [{"code": "E_CHECK_NOTES", "message": error}], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
            else { eprintln!("check: E_CHECK_NOTES: {error}"); }
            return 2;
        }
    } };
    let acknowledgment_not_applied = (security.refuted() && options.flags.contains_key("ack-security")).then_some(
        "--ack-security was not applied: a security invariant is refuted; an acknowledgment records specification rationale for a review note and cannot override a concrete counterexample. Repair the policy or the invariant and rerun check.");
    if !options.json { if let Some(message) = acknowledgment_not_applied { eprintln!("check: {message}"); } }
    let evidence=security::witnesses::collect(&project,&bound,&compiled_sources,&security.notes,true,options);
    let dispositions = match security_reviews::review_evidence(&project,&compiled_sources,&security.notes,
        if security.refuted(){None}else{options.flags.get("ack-security").map(|id|(id.as_str(),options.flags.get("reason").map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("")))},&evidence) {
        Ok(d)=>d,Err(error)=>{if options.json {println!("{}",json!({"ok":false,"security":&security,"problems":[{"code":"E_SECURITY_NOTES","message":error}]}));}else{eprintln!("check: E_SECURITY_NOTES: {error}");}return 2;}
    };
    check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json, Some((&security, &dispositions)));
    check_surface::remember(&project, &bound, Some((&security, &dispositions)));
    let security_failed=security.refuted()||security_reviews::pending(&dispositions);
    let visible_security=security.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).filter(|(_,d)|!d.acknowledged).map(|(n,_)|n).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let compact_security=snapback4_lang::security::questionnaire_notes(&visible_security.iter().filter(|n|crate::note_rank::tier(&n.code)!=crate::note_rank::Tier::Review).map(|n|(*n).clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>());
    let present = |text: &str| crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, text, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let review = options.flags.contains_key("review") || options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("all");
    let details = review && (acquisition.is_none() || options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
    let certificates: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().map(|p| crate::scale::analyze_bound(&bound.schema, p)).collect();
    let scale_summary = crate::scale::summary(&certificates);
    let note_count = compact_security.len() + reviewed.new.len() + certificates.iter().map(|certificate| certificate.search.len()).sum::<usize>();
    let field_authority: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name,table)| table.written_by.iter().map(move |(column,authority)|
        format!("{name}.{column}: written by .{}; visibility: {}", authority.owner, if authority.audience { "readable by audience" } else { "private to owner" }))).collect();
    let row_views = bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter_map(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().map(|k| json!({
        "view": m.target, "source": m.source, "sourceKey": k.source_key, "member": k.table,
        "memberKey": k.member_key, "fanout": k.fanout, "range": row_view_range(&bound.schema, m), "writerRecount": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::WRITER_RECOUNT, "recountChunk": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::RECOUNT_CHUNK
    }))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let elapsed = started.elapsed();
    let synced = bound.schema.synced().count();
    let sequences: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name, table)| table.sequences.iter().map(move |(column, reference)| {
        format!("sequence {name}.{column}: counter per {} row through .{reference}; immutable, provisional while pending", table.columns[reference])
    })).collect();
    if options.json {
        println!("{}", json!({ "ok": !security_failed, "acknowledgmentNotApplied": acknowledgment_not_applied, "securityReviews": &dispositions, "security": &security, "followingHistory": bound.programs.iter().filter_map(|p| crate::scale::following::certificate(&bound.schema, &p.program)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "rowViews": row_views, "fieldAuthority": field_authority, "sequences": sequences, "scaleSummary": scale_summary, "search": certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "plans": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(bound.programs.iter().map(|p| (&p.program.name, p.selections())).collect::<BTreeMap<_, _>>()) } else { None }, "scale": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(&certificates) } else { None }, "tables": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).count(), "views": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some()).count(), "synced": synced, "operations": bound.programs.len(), "heldQueries": bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, fact)| fact.held).map(|(name, _)| name).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": reviewed.new, "acknowledgedNotes": reviewed.acknowledged, "predictionSummary": snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_summary(&bound), "starterLeftovers": init::starter_leftovers_json(&project.root), "acquisition": acquisition, "residency": if options.flags.contains_key("residency") { Some(residency::facts(&bound)) } else { None }, "native": native.as_ref().map(|b| json!({ "module": b.path, "bytes": b.bytes, "jobs": b.jobs, "ms": b.ms })), "ms": elapsed.as_millis() }));
    } else {
        println!("{}",crate::presentation::check_verdict(security_failed,&table_counts(&bound.schema),synced,bound.programs.len(),if security.refuted(){"security invariant refuted"}else if security_failed{"security review required"}else{"compiled"},dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.gated&&!d.acknowledged).count(),dispositions.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&!d.acknowledged&&d.witness.is_some()).count(),dispositions.iter().filter(|d|!d.gated&&!d.acknowledged&&d.witness.is_none()).count(),dispositions.iter().filter(|d|d.acknowledged).count(),note_count,elapsed.as_secs_f64()*1000.0,init::client_setup_hint(&project.root),&native.as_ref().map(|b|format!("; native: {} → {} ({} B, {} ms)",b.jobs.join(", "),b.path.display(),b.bytes,b.ms)).unwrap_or_default()));
        security::enforced(&security,&bound);
        if review && !reviewed.acknowledged.is_empty() { println!("{} reviewed notes (check --verbose to inspect); source edits require review again", reviewed.acknowledged.len()); }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            for line in &sequences { println!("  {line}"); }
            for (name, _) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_some()) {
                println!("  {name}: {}", snapback4_lang::bind::residency::table_line(&bound.schema, name));
            }
            for (name, fact) in bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, f)| f.ephemeral) {
                println!("{}", present(&snapback4_lang::bind::residency::operation_lines(name, fact)));
            }
        }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") { for line in &field_authority { println!("  {line}"); } }
        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
            // Verbose keeps every explanation, in the same consequence order as the summary.
            let mut ranked: Vec<&snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = bound.notes.iter().collect();
            ranked.sort_by_key(|note| crate::note_rank::tier(&note.code));
            let ranked: Vec<snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = ranked.into_iter().cloned().collect();
            for report in note_reports(&ranked, true) { println!("{}", present(&report)); }
        } else if !review {
            for note in &reviewed.new {
                if crate::note_rank::tier(&note.code)==crate::note_rank::Tier::Fix {
                    println!("fix: {} {}: {} — check --review for repair",note.site,note.code,present(&note_summary(note)));
                }
            }
        } else if review {
            // Notes are advisory: ranked and grouped after the ok line, repeats shortened (note_rank).
            let mut notes = reviewed.new.iter().chain(compact_security.iter()).map(|note| {
                let compact = completeness_note(note);
                crate::note_rank::Note { site: note.site.to_string(), code: note.code.clone(), summary: present(&note_summary(note)),
                    form: compact.is_none().then(|| crate::note_rank::compiled_form(&note.code, &note.rewrite)).flatten().map(|(lead, code)| (lead, present(&code))), compact }
            })
                .chain(certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).map(|search| crate::note_rank::Note { site: search.site.to_string(), code: search.code.to_string(), summary: present(search.summary), form: None, compact: None }))
                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
            notes.sort_by_key(|note|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&note.code),crate::note_rank::site_order(&note.site),note.code.clone(),note.summary.clone()));
            let total=notes.len();crate::note_rank::shortlist(&mut notes);
            let remaining=total-notes.len();
            if remaining>0 {println!("{remaining} more actionable notes in check --verbose/JSON");}
            for line in crate::note_rank::render(notes) { println!("{}", crate::presentation::abbreviate_quoted(&line)); }

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A device per-query result cache.** No range receipts, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7). The server's narrow Postgres revision-equality cache and explicitly clock-qualified G6 read grant are the exceptions below; cross-revision promotion remains excluded.
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres deploy/serve admits the initial Unix-socket HTTP profile under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20; `guide release-json`). Provider HA, full parity and publication remain outside that profile.

## Moves

- **2026-10-24 — a bounded server read grant moves onto the doing-list** (author's G6 commission, LLP 3000.009.001.000.002 §4.2). It unblocks zero-flight Postgres hits after G5's measured validation CPU limit. Taken off the doing-list: mandatory per-hit database validation for the explicitly qualified, single-owner Linux deployment. Unqualified clocks/failover and lost/uncertain epochs use G5; device result caches and cross-revision promotion remain excluded.

- **2026-10-24 — Postgres server revision-equality read reuse** (LLP
  3000.009.001.000.002 §4/§6 G5). This removes repeated interpretation and
  data decoding for eligible pure queries at an unchanged durable revision.
  Taken off: the blanket server result-cache exclusion. Every reuse still
  validates fresh authority, revision and code in one database statement;
  SQLite stays on ordinary reads because it has no complete revision stream.
  Device caches, dependency promotion, retained authority and zero-flight
  grants stay excluded. Operator-local counters add no HTTP or authoring API.

- **2026-10-20 — Snapback 4 on Postgres moves onto the doing-list** (author: "commit and push these things, then do your \"what i'd do next\" things, then get this working on postgres and measure it"; LLP 3000.009.001 and its children). Unblocks: the store-port extraction on SQLite (P0, including the `Committer` seam with the serial batch); the Postgres adapters for every durable-state family and `serve`/`deploy`/`doctor` over Postgres (P1), with store-held fencing, the declared HA mode and `BlobStore`; the optimistic committer, commissioned on 2026-10-19 and built after its execution stage is sized; the scale program of LLP 3000.009.001.002 with its raw-Postgres, Supabase and Convex controls on fleet machines; and a research note comparing replicated SQLite with Postgres for applications that outgrow one SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation-only framing of the Postgres adapter. Stands: no publication or managed-service deployment authority, no lowering of Quarry to SQL, no SQL writes to managed data, one core owner. Several serving processes (P2), log retention, live graduation (LLP 3000.009.001.001) and the async-safe external anchor each wait for their own Moves line.

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

### 2026-10-26 — DX12: short release output

Deploy uses DX11's render_mode: gates/refutations remain actionable; advisory
decisions are counted and expanded with --review. Activation leads with verdict
then REL's live identity and keeps validation previews behind --verbose.
Releases is compact by default and retains structured evidence with --json.

median complete stdout+stderr is 33 lines with published 0.4.42 and 5 with DX12.

## 2026-10-26 — REL2 release identity and wave-29 refusals

REL2 makes a fitting one-child reverse projection eligible for original-source
selection despite current invariants or declaration layout. The closed proof
retains authored guards, invariant content, shape/helper meaning and write
payloads; the writer requires a validated parent projection for every child and
parent. A v2-born singleton can establish that receipt atomically; a competing
child cannot. Previews and live release JSON disclose bound predecessor result
shape differences. Historical source reactivation reuses its source/manifest;
latest-source compiler registry upgrades retain the existing qualification path.
List-bound inclusion and self-baseline saving remove 008/011's false refusals.
Roster provisioning uses a kernel writer lock plus a diagnostic holder lease,
including per-attempt fencing of delayed identity requests. See LLP 3000.000.000
§22 and the verbatim dual-family reviews in `llp/reviews/rel2-wave29/`.

The final affected sweep caught a source-origin cutoff which refused restoration
of rows made dormant by intervening narrowing. Reactivation now preserves the
complete committed rollback closure. Equivalent qualification targets share
registry coverage by executable digest, guarded bytes and hiding policy;
alternating A/B deploys stabilize, and alias lookup cannot borrow a different
hiding acknowledgment. CLI regressions cover restoration, repeated deployment,
every retained target and a mismatched legacy policy. Changelog verb ownership
and the unchanged non-delivery await classification are checked explicitly.

## 2026-10-26 — G7-E fix-forward: terminal completion and PG17 certificate timeout

On integrated main `bcf780d307`, pause rejection could publish a second
terminal outcome after ownership loss had already filled a command's reply
channel. With recovery parked, the new witness enqueues a program and a
solo command, loses ownership before consuming either reply, supersedes the
recovery epoch, and drops the queue while both replies remain unread. The
before implementation fails with `Queue::drop deadlocked on a second
terminal reply`. Every program and solo terminal path now shares one
completion sender: ownership loss, pause rejection, unknown outcomes,
prepare/continuation panics and certified results atomically take it once
and send without blocking. Reading a result cannot reopen completion;
started work keeps its actual result.

Marker and recovery certificates share settings that locally disable
statement/lock timeouts and conditionally execute `SET LOCAL
transaction_timeout=0` on PG17 and later. PG16 never executes that setting.
The conditional stays in the existing certificate message and its command
tag joins the marker's exact validation. On a separate private PG17
cluster, both new deferred-COMMIT witnesses sleep 600 ms with a 300 ms
session transaction timeout. Both fail before with unknown outcomes and
server `terminating connection due to transaction timeout` records. The
protected PG17 cluster on port 55440 is untouched. No throughput campaign
or push is part of this lane; raw evidence remains under `/tmp/g7eff-*`.


Final checks: full core **1,311/0** (526 ignored), full
binary **626/0** (75 ignored), E core/HTTP **40/0** and **5/0**,
private PG17 **2/0**, G7.2 **40/0**, RT0 core/HTTP **6/0** and **10/0**,
crash/kill **11/0** with zero acknowledged loss or lost-history release.
Full binary build and public serving pass. Postgres Rust targets are
**817/1** across 26 targets: only inherited `E_INPUT: 6`; the inherited
stale evaluator lockfile blocks its known `40001` stage. No additional red.
Ref-check: **569 LLPs, 12,045 references, zero errors/unchecked**.
All 7 private cluster directories confirm stopped; the combined
8,020-pair audit, public PID ledger, test drivers and target executable
scan find **no PIDs left**, including zombies. Minimum free disk is
45.63 GiB. The ticket is resolved and moved to `issues/closed/`;
the complete evidence is in [the G7-E report](reports/20261025-g7e-postgres/REPORT.md#g7-e-fix-forward--terminal-completion-and-pg17-transaction-timeout).
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.
